 2. A. 05.21.19 
2. RECOGNITION 
A. Mission Moment – Wass Wolves’ Wellness Night 
As t his evening’s Mission Moment , we would like to take th e opportunity to 
recognize Wass Elementary School.  In January, the Wass PTO sponsored the 
Wass Wolves’ Wellness Night.   Wass provides a learning night for its students 
every year. Over the last few years, Wass has held learning nights that focus on 
science, literacy, math, and visual arts. The focus this y ear was to provide 
students and parents with an educational program that emphasizes living a 
healthy lifestyle physically, nutritionally, and social -emotionally.  The program 
aligned with the Troy School District’s Red Pillar of “Attending to the Social -
Emotional and Physical Well -Being of All.”   Mark Cavataio, physical education 
teacher at Wass Elementary, is here to share more on this world class Red Pillar 
event.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Invite Mark Cavataio up to share a bit more 
about this program. 
Student Speakers: 
Laura Witherspoon 
Victor Pertile 
Sahana Nair

 2. B. 05.21.19 
2.       RECOGNITION 
B.   Troy School District’s Athletics CAP Pact 
The Coach-Athlete-Parent (CAP) Pact is an expectations model that was 
finalized by the Troy Athletic Advisory Committee (TAAC).  TAAC is 
comprised of four high school athletes, four parents of high school athletes, 
four high school coaches, and athletic directors.  Over the course of winter 
and spring meetings, this committee’s collaborative work and efforts 
molded a generic athletics expectations model originally created by 
Growing Great Relationships into our district’s model.  This expectations 
model will now serve to guide how coaches, athletes, and parents interact 
and operate.  The TSD CAP Pact will be implemented in the upcoming fall 
sports season.  Thank you to our TAAC members for their investment in 
the process and development of the CAP Pact. 
 Athens HS Troy HS 
AD Bob Dowd Dan House 
Coach Todd Heugh Brian Miska 
Coach Stacie Klumpp Chris Frasier 
Parent Colleen Lindgren Ross Parpart

Board of Education Meeting
5
CAP PACT
• Developed by Growing Great Relationships, 
LLC
• Results from parent, athlete, coach surveys
• 5 sections relating expectations between two 
parties
• Complaint/Grievance Procedure
2
6
CAP PACT Core Principles
• Coach,  Athlete and Parent are in partnership 
• Partnerships are built on honest, open 
communication
• Communication is two-way and based upon 
mutual respect 
• Disputes are resolved according to specific written 
procedures 
3
7
CAP PACT Sections
• Coach’s Expectations of Parents
• Parent’s Expectations of Coach
• Coach’s Expectations of Athlete
• Athlete’s Expectations of Coach
• Athlete’s Expectations of Parents
• Defined Grievance Policy
4
8
TSD CAP PACT Implementation
• Share with parents and athletes at pre-season 
parent meeting
• All parties sign the document (will be a required 
form for participation)
• Coach can utilize in discussions with team, 
individuals
• Begin in Fall 2019

2. RECOGNITION 
C.  2018-19 Outstanding Support Staff Award    
The Troy School District has many outstanding support staff employees. This evening we 
are proud to recognize the accomplishments of 25 individuals who were nominated for the 
Troy School District Outstanding Support Staff Award. 
The nominees were chosen for this distinction by their colleagues, by parents and by former 
and current students, because they make a difference for our students each and every day. 
They exemplify our values and help us to ensure that when we say we ensure learning for 
all - ALL truly means ALL. 
We are grateful for their dedication and passion for our students. 
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Distribute 
certificates/packets/plants.)

2.       RECOGNITION 
 E.  National Teacher Appreciation Week 
Teacher Appreciation Week is observed nationally each year in May. This year, 
Teacher Appreciation Week was celebrated May 6th – May 10th.  At tonight’s 
meeting, the Troy School District and Board of Education would like to 
recognize all of our outstanding teachers by adopting the following resolution: 
WHEREAS, throughout Troy and across the country, teachers inspire, 
nurture and open children’s minds to new ideas,  
WHEREAS, teachers promote and strengthen the principles of democratic 
equality, develop leaders and encourage critical thinking; and 
WHEREAS, Troy teachers set the bar for excellence in education and 
continually exceed the highest standards in the profession; and 
WHEREAS, the goal of Teacher Appreciation Week is to raise national and 
local awareness of teaching excellence in building a better society; now,  
THEREFORE BE IT  RESOLVED, that the Troy School District supports 
National Teacher Appreciation Week, May 6-10, 2019, and encourages our 
schools and community to appropriately recognize the vital and changing role 
of teachers in imagining and creating a better future for society. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution. 
  Moved by:     
  Supported by:

on  its  staff.  This  evening,  the  Board  recognized  the 
accomplishments of 43 individuals who were nominated 
for  the  Troy  School  District  Outstanding Teacher  Award 
Program.  The nominees represent exemplary educators 
at the elementary, middle and high school levels. They 
were  nominated  by  their  peers,  parents  and  by 
students.    These  teachers  have  had  a  tremendous 
impact on the lives of so many through their dedication 
and  commitment  to  their  students.  Their  nomination

     5. D.         05.21.19 
5. CONSENT 
 D. CONSIDERATION:  Approve Oakland Intermediate School 
District 2019-2020 Budget 
  In accordance with Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, 
the Troy Board of Education is required to review the proposed Oakland 
Intermediate School District budget and express either its support or 
disapproval of this budget.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
  Moved by  
  Supported by  
WHEREAS: 
 1. Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, requires the intermediate 
school board to submit its proposed budget no later than May 1 of each year to 
the board of each constituent district for review; and 
 2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent district shall 
review the proposed intermediate school district budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or disapproval of the proposed intermediate 
school district budget, and shall submit to the intermediate school board any 
specific objections and proposed changes the constituent district board has to 
the budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Troy Board of Education has received and reviewed the proposed 
intermediate school district budget in accordance with Section 624 of the 
Revised School Code, as amended, and, by the adoption of this resolution, 
expresses its support for the proposed intermediate school district budget.  The 
Troy School District Board of Education believes that it is the responsibility of 
143
the Intermediate School District to be responsible for its own operations, 
including adoption of its budget.  The Troy School District’s s upport of the 
Oakland Intermediate School District’s budget is for the sole purpose of 
complying with Section 624 of the Revised School Code. 
 2. The secretary of the Troy Board of Education or his/her designee shall forward 
a copy of this resolution to the intermediate school board or its superintendent 
no later than June 1, 2019. 
 3. All resolutions insofar as they conflict with this resolution be and the same are 
hereby rescinded. 
Yes:  
No:  
Resolution declared adopted. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education 
The undersigned duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of Education of the 
Troy School District, Michigan, hereby certifies that the foregoing is a true and 
complete copy of a resolution adopted by the Troy Board of Education at a regular 
meeting held on May 21, 2019, the original of which resolution is part of the Board’s 
minutes, and further certifies that notice of the meeting was given to the public under 
the Open Meetings Act, 1976 PA 267, as amended. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education

WHEREAS, Michigan law provides that every local unit of government, including 
a school district, shall hold a public hearing on its proposed budget, with notice 
of such at least six (6) days before the hearing; and 
WHEREAS, such notice is required to include the time and place of the hearing 
and also state the place where a copy of the proposed budget is available for 
public inspection; and 
WHEREAS, the statutory provision a lso requires that the notice include in 11 -
point bold faced type, the following statement:   
THE PROPERTY TAX MILLAGE RATE PROPOSED TO BE LEVIED TO 
SUPPORT THE PROPOSED BUDGET WILL BE A SUBJECT OF THIS 
HEARING.

5. E.    5.21.19 
WHEREAS, each local unit is required to hold the public hearing prior to 
final adoption of its budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
1. A public hearing shall be held on June 11, 2019 at a special Board 
meeting beginning at 5:00 p.m. at the Troy School District Services 
Building, 4420 Livernois Road, Troy 48098, for the purpose of 
receiving testimony and discussing the proposed 2019-2020 budget. 
2. The Secretary of the Board of Education is hereby authorized and 
directed to publish notice of such public hearing, said notice to be in 
substantially the form of attached Exhibit A, in a newspaper of 
general circulation within the School Distric t.  The notice shall be 
published at least six (6) days before the public hearing and shall 
comply with all requirements of the applicable statutory provisions.

6. A.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment –  
     Director, College & Career Programming 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Mr. William Turner  to the 
position of Director, College & Career Programming.   Mr. Turner has an 
extensive 19-year career in education.  He began h is career as a teacher for 
Utica High School .  M r. Turner then served as a Counselor at Jeannette Junior 
High School in Sterling Heights  and then at Stevenson High School in Sterling 
Heights.  His career with the Troy School District began as a Counselor at Troy 
High School.  Mr. Turner has also served as the Night Supervisor for the Troy 
School District Adult Education Department and the Principal for the Troy School 
District Summer School.  Currently, Mr. Turner has served the last seven years as 
Assistant Principal at Athens High School.  During his career  he has served as a 
district team member for New P edagogies for Deep Learning Cohort and the 
head of the District Vision Team for Career and Technical Education.  Mr. Turner 
has also served as a mentor to fiv e different teacher leaders asp iring to be 
administrators.  Mr. Turner has a Bachelor of Science Degree in English from 
Michigan State University , a Master of Arts in S chool Counseling fr om Sienna 
Heights College , and a n Education Specialist Degree in Education Leadership 
from Saginaw Valley University.   
With your approval, Mr. Turner will begin his duties as Director, College & Career 
Programming as of July 1, 2019.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below:  
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION

 Supported by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Board of Education issues a two-year 
probationary administrative contract without tenure in the administrative position 
to Mr. William Turner, who will begin his duties as Director, College & Career 
Programming as of July 1, 2019.

Secondary Science Materials Adoption: High School Science May 2019 Submitted by Kate Rainey and High School Science Teachers  Rationale This materials adoption is a part of the Troy School District regular seven-year adoption cycle.  Our current secondary science materials are out of date and are not aligned with the Michigan Science Standards approved by the State of Michigan School Board (2015). The Michigan Science Standards are a state-approved version of the Next Generation Science Standards (NGSS).    Current research indicates that students learn science by engaging in work as scientists. In a highly effective learning environment, students work collaboratively to develop evidence-based explanations of real-life phenomena that create a cohesive and compelling storyline. Classrooms are shifting away from places where students learn about science by memorizing facts to places where students figure out science by generating questions, designing investigations, analyzing evidence, and communicating their findings to others (See Figure 1. A New Vision for Science Education).   Process of Materials Selection Our process of materials adoption really started years ago when we began professional learning around the expectations of the new science standards. All high school teachers were engaged in reviewing texts and laboratory materials. The EQUIP rubric, published by Achieve and aligned to NGSS, was used to evaluate text materials. The review process took place over a full year and many teachers tested lessons and equipment in their classrooms. Priority was given to materials that are aligned to the NGSS and allow students the opportunity to investigate phenomena through compelling storylines.    Features of Recommended Texts and Equipment In March 2019, all teachers were asked to develop a list of features of the text series and equipment they were recommending as well as to provide a rationale for why other texts/materials were not chosen.  The features of the resources reviewed, piloted and recommended, are summarized in Table 1 (pages 4-19).    Textbook Recommendation Each course group came to consensus around text and material choices, and the recommended materials are summarized in Table 2 (pages 19 and 20) with projected costs.  Publisher quotes are included at the end of this document.  Copies of select texts from each series are available for review in the Teaching and Learning Workroom in the Services Building.    
175
Figure 1:  A New Vision for Science Education 
 http://ngss.nsta.org/Documents/New%20Vision%20for%20Science%20Education.pdf    
3  
176
4  
Table 1:  Features of Recommended Texts and Texts Considered   Biology  Recommended Text: Other Texts Considered:  Miller & Levine Biology, 2019, Pearson • Detailed and readable resource for students to reference content introduced in class and support mastery of the disciplinary core ideas (DCIs)  • Case studies are used to introduce each chapter and provide opportunities for students to design their own solutions to problems  • Guided inquiry projects and labs • Detailed graphs and diagrams that can be used to build student understandings     
BSCS Biology: A Human Approach, 2016, Kendall Hunt Publishing Co. • Phenomena based and open-ended design investigations • Rigid storylines that would require a complete overhaul of our curriculum Holt McDougal Biology, 2012, HMH • Case studies anchor each chapter • Flow of the text does not allow easy access for students Glencoe Biology, 2017, McGraw Hill • Good reference text • Lacking in real-life applications Inspire Science Biology, 2020, McGraw Hill • 3Dimensions are evident in each chapter • Connections to phenomena are weak Biology for NGSS, 2016, Biozone • Data-rich activities for students that allow for 3-Dimensional learning • Not a comprehensive text and too cost restrictive to purchase as an additional resource Recommended Materials:  Other Materials Considered:  PASCO molecular model kit • Allow students to create visual representations of molecules • Easy to manipulate, including creating single and double bonds      
Lab-Aids kits • Poor quality and not durable • Pieces cannot be taken apart

5  
Chemistry 1  Recommended Text: Other Texts Considered:  No adoption at this time • Only one textbook we previewed was aligned to the NGSS, however, it lacks a storyline and any true phenomena. The textbook is still in development, and we were unable to pilot and explore these features.  • We are interested in learning more about the open source chemistry curricula currently being developed by Michigan State University, CREATE for STEM, that is planned to be released next year.  
None of the below text series below are aligned to NGSS • Glencoe Chemistry: Matter & Change, 2017, McGraw Hill • Pearson Chemistry, 2017, Pearson • Essential Chemistry, 2018, PASCO Education • World of Chemistry, 2013, Cengage Learning • Inspire Chemistry, 2020, McGraw Hill • Living by Chemistry, 2015, bfw Freeman 
Recommended Materials:  Materials Piloted:  Due to the fact that we have not been able to identify a textbook or program that adequately supports our curriculum and the NGSS, we feel it is most appropriate to wait on new probeware as well. In addition, there is the opportunity to obtain probeware via TFEE grants.    
PASCO and Vernier probeware and sensors were piloted.  
Active Chemistry/Earth Science  Recommended Text: Other Texts Considered:  ChemMatters: Demystifying Everyday Chemistry, ACS Chemistry for Life periodical • Everyday phenomena - provides current, real-world based articles that gives students the opportunity to connect chemistry to their own lives • Sufficiently challenging reading level allows all students to access the information • Supplemental - compliments the content and reading in the Active Chemistry series • Engaging and enriching - many of the articles connect to lab experiences and enhance students’ learning beyond the lab 
Active Chemistry: Project-Based Inquiry Approach, 2011, It’s About Time • Engaging activities and labs that provide real-world connections for students • The updates to the latest version do not include any features we find necessary at this time • Our class sets of this text series (2007) are in good condition and do not need to be replaced  
178
6  
Physics 1  Recommended Text: Other Texts Considered:  Inspire Science: Physics, 2020, McGraw Hill • Alignment to the NGSS • The progression closely follows the storylines we’ve developed  • Reinforces concepts, and is a readable and detailed text that students can use as a resource  
Pearson Physics, 2014, Pearson • Attempts to connect to real-world experiences • Text is unclear and difficult to use Physics: Principles with Applications, 2014, Pearson • Solutions easily found online • Lacks example problems In addition to investigating text series the Physics teachers took a deep dive into equipment. To align our Physics 1 curriculum with the NGSS, we have taken strides to provide our students more opportunities to engage in constructing conceptual models. In practice, this means students are collecting data from experiments they conduct and at times design themselves. Through this process it has become apparent that our current stock of equipment needs to be updated to allow students the flexibility to make experimental design decisions that are suited for their proposed experiments.  Please refer to the Appendix to view an example of a Physics storyline.  Currently, we have a mix of Pasco and Vernier equipment and probeware. We have used both successfully, but we have found that Vernier has some advantages in key areas that ultimately make it more flexible and more user friendly for students. See below.   
 
Vernier Equipment PASCO Equipment Battery operated (portable) Requires power source (not portable) Data analysis occurs on LabQuest (no computer required) Data analysis on PASCO software (computer required) LabQuest streams data directly to other devices  Adapter required to stream data to other devices Companion app (Graphical Analysis) works consistently and is user friendly Companion app (Spark View) crashed often and was difficult to use Students can stream data that can be viewed and analyzed on multiple devices  Students can stream data that can be viewed but not analyzed on multiple devices Will work well with either of the possible 1:1 devices (iPad or Windows laptop) Will work best with one of the possible 1:1 devices (Windows laptop) 
179
7  
Equipment Recommended: Other Equipment Considered:  PASCO ripple generator and ripple tank (AHS, THS) • Ripple Tanks are used to create and visualize 3-dimensional surface waves by projecting them on a 2-dimensional screen. • By varying amplitude, frequency, and the characteristics of the medium, students can participate in discussions of wave propagation, reflection, refraction, diffraction, and the Doppler Effect. PASCO projectile launcher system* (AHS and TCCHS) • Allows students to explore and model 2D motion and projectile objects and illustrates the idea that motion in different dimensions is absolutely independent.  Vernier go direct sensor cart and masses (AHS) • Low friction carts allow us to investigate linear motion without noticeable slowing due to friction.  • These dynamics carts are also enabled with springs and magnets which provide opportunities to investigate collisions, forces, and conservation concepts. Genetics, Bacteriology, Biotechnology and Embryology  Recommended Text: Other Texts Considered:  Human Genetics: Concepts and Application, 2018, McGraw Hill • Updated genetic concepts supported by real-world phenomena • Case studies, graphics and question sets provide opportunities for students to deepen their understanding of genetics • This newest edition includes essential topics such as cancer and biotechnology

Recommended Materials:  Egg Incubator • A hatching incubator is integral to the embryology unit of GBBE  • The dual system provides an incubator for 1-2 hatchings to go to term where students can candle the eggs throughout the process to observe development, then another incubator for short term development where students make their own slides from the embryos.  • Students draw comparisons between Aves, Mammals, and organisms with two-chambered hearts, and observe how the development of the embryos mirror the history of vertebrates and evolutionary development of the mammalian four-chambered heart.  Bacteria Incubator  • The biotechnology and bacteriology aspects of the GBBE curriculum rely on the use of incubators in student experimentations  • During the bacterial gene transformation lab, students explore how gene transformations are used as medicine to improve the lives of those suffering from diseases or to mass produce hormones • In the three-week Unknown Laboratory, students receive two Eppendorf tubes identified as “patient A” and “patient B”.  Their objective is to determine each patient’s bacterial infection while keeping the bacteria alive and testing for morphological and metabolic characteristics.  Students present their findings after they have identified the infection. Astronomy  Recommended Text: None. Through the use of equipment, specifically telescopes, students will have the opportunity to deepen their learning in Astronomy.    Other Texts Considered: Discovering the Universe, 2014, W.H. Freeman and Company • Is updated with some, but not all, current missions to planets/moons/asteroids • Not very visual and is text heavy  • Very dry / not very exciting for students to read. Explorations: An Introduction to Astronomy, 2017, McGraw Hill • Vivid, colorful images and diagrams • Doesn't break ideas down to an appropriate level for our students.  As a team, we researched telescopes extensively and created a plan for their use with students. Viewing objects with a telescope with your own eyes is a very different experience than seeing images online, and so telescopes are an invaluable resource for students.  • The telescopes will be used with a solar filter (we have one at each school) to view sunspots on the sun during the day • The telescopes will be used to study the Moon during the day  
188
16  
• Telescopes will allow students to gain practical knowledge of the function of different types of telescopes (reflectors, refractors, and compound) • Most importantly, our high schools will have common observing nights a few times a semester so students can come out in the evening (at least two teachers will be present, and parents/guardians must be present with the students) to see planets, Jupiter's moons, double stars, nebulae, etc.) Recommended Equipment: Other Equipment Considered:  The telescopes below are high quality, durable and highly recommended on astronomy forums such as cloudynights.com and Sky & Telescope Magazine  Zhumell Z8 Deluxe Dobsonian Reflector Telescope  • Large aperture allows students to observe how deep sky objects such as nebulas, galaxies, globular clusters, etc. appear fainter • Allows students to explore how a Reflector telescope works and to compare it to other types of scopes • Easy for students to use on their own • Roller bearing allows it to move more smoothly than the Orion SkyQuest (that uses Teflon pads) and a cooling fan allows it to acclimate optics to the temperature of the surroundings • Dual speed focuser – course and fine focusers Celestron 1.25” Zoom Eyepiece 8-24mm  • Allows students to zoom in on objects in the telescope without changing eyepieces and losing the object Meade Instruments Infinity 90 mm AZ Refractor Telescope  • Gives students the opportunity to view the appearance of planets, double stars, and open clusters  • Quick and easy to setup and for students to use 
Orion SkyQuest XT8 Dobsonian Reflector Telescope • Has a large aperture • Easy for students to locate objects • Is a little jerky to move due to the Teflon pads vs the Zhumell’s roller bearings • No cooling fan to acclimate optics to temperature of surroundings • No dual speed focuser – only one knob – no fine control TwinStar AstroMark 50mm 75x Power Refractor • Cost effective • Optics not recommended by reviews – poor views of objects TwinStar AstroMark 50mm 75x Power Refractor  • Cost effective • Optics not recommended by reviews – poor views of objects Vixen 5835 125 mm Binocular Telescope  • Good optics • Very expensive ($4000)

Appendix:   PHYSICS 1 Storyline  Anchoring Phenomenon: How can we protect an egg/cell phone from a fall? Student Question  Equipment Used What students do What evidence students collect Students’ answers 
What is the acceleration of a falling object? LabQuest 2 Motion Sensors Objects  iPad/Video Analysis 
Students use motion sensor in combination with the labquest to determine the acceleration of a falling object.  Video record the object falling against a scaled background.  
Students look at the slope of the velocity time graph on the labquest to determine the acceleration.  Students collect data of position and time to determine the acceleration of the object.   
I know the acceleration of the egg as it falls.   I know there are unbalanced forces acting on the egg to cause the acceleration.  
What’s the maximum force of impact? Force Sensors Force Plates Accelerometers LabQuest 2 
Students use the force plate in combination with the labquest to collect Force vs. time data for an impact.  
Students look at the maximum force reading on the force vs. time graph to determine the maximum acceleration of the egg during the impact. 
I know the force that the egg will hit the ground at.  
What is the change in momentum/Impulse during the collision?  
2.2 m track 1.1 m track LabQuest 2 Motion Sensors Force Sensor 
Students analyze collisions between two cars on ramps and flat tracks. Students collect 
Students look at the area of the Force vs. time graph to 
I can calculate the change in momentum and impulse from the data

The graph produced during collision identifies the time component of impulse.

Subject: ECC Projector, Paging and Classroom Audio Bid Pack #9884 Recommendation Letter 
Beth, 
We received, opened, and publicly read bids and proposals for projectors, paging and classroom audio 
systems for the new Early Childhood Center on April 25, 2019 at 2:00 P.M.  The following is a summary 
of the evaluation and our recommendation. 
Five proposals were received.  The initial results were tabulated as follows: 
Contractor Paging Classroom Audio Projectors 
Sound Planning 44,984.00 25,458.00 44,749.00 
All Covered -- 35,736.39 34,511.85 
Data Image -- -- 35,289.00 
Amcomm 35,000.00 50,000.00 60,000.00 
VSC 37,252.10 12,350.00 -- 
SoundCom 44,981.00 39,451.00 63,648.00 
DataCom 39,875.00 37,615.00 64,115.00 
Vendors were advised as part of the bid process that the District may award the above projects 
separately and that vendors could bid any or all of the projects. 
All proposals were evaluated for compliance with the RFP requirements, pricing, technical solution and 
acceptance of terms and conditions of the RFP. All Bidders provided compliant proposals except VSC 
who bid a non-compliant product for their base bid classroom audio but proposed a compliant product 
as a voluntary alternate.  The voluntary alternate raised the Classroom Audio pricing to $40,131.00.  
Sound Planning also provided a voluntary alternate that the District would choose to accept to bring in a 
product and manufacturer that is in use in the District today.  That would bring the Sound Planning 
proposal up to $27,483.00 for the classroom audio. 
Basedon our evaluation, the decision was made to split the awards giving the Projector project to All 
Covered who is the low bidder on that project and provided a 5 year warranty on the Epson projector 
product.  We further recommend awarding the Paging and Classroom Audio projects together to 
208
DataCom.  It makes the most sense to award thee two projects to one vendor as the systems are tied 
together for specific functions.   
DataCom is the the second low bidder on these combined projects. There are several reasons why 
DataCom was chosen over the low bidder.  First, the product proposed for the Classroom Audio is 
consistant with products being used in the District today.  Second, the paging system proposed by the 
DataCom Group has a feature set specific to education that more closely aligns with the future safety 
and security plans of the District.  These features include: 
 Pre-recorded announcements 
 Use and storage of WAV files 
 Lockdown and emergency notification 
 Unified/Mass notification 
 Blue light stations 
 Desktop alerting 
 Ability to communicate out to mobile devices 
 Ability to integrate with existing intercom systems 
The pricing for the two low bidders for paging and classroom audio is a difference of approximately 
$5,000.00 and is outlined below. 
The DataCom Group $77,490.00 
Sound Planning $72,467.00 
For the reasons stated above, it is our recommendation to award these contracts as follows: 
All Covered   Projector Project  $  34,511.85

Memorandum 
To:  Rick West, Troy Schools 
From:  Christa Amalio, Barton Malow Co. 
CC:  Michelle Kerns-LeCole Planners, Steve Rewers-BMC, Rob Carson-TSD 
Date:  May 21, 2019 
On March 1, 2018 bids were received for the Troy Schools ECC project.  
Titan Retail Development was the low bidder for the General 
Trades/Carpentry bid package.  Titan was interviewed on March 6, 2019, 
during the post bid interview the scope of work was reviewed including 
metal trusses, wood windows, exterior walls of the building, doors, and 
trim. 
On April 25th 2019, Titan requested a meeting with BMC where they 
expressed that the felt the wood trim and wood door frames were not in 
their scope due to lack of specified spec section number on their work 
scope document. After the meeting support documents were retrieved 
from bid time, see attached 
Due to the schedule and the need to have material for the wood trim and 
door frames on site as soon as possible, we rebid the scope and received 
bids on May 16th.  Three bids were received.  The 2nd bidder, Barton 
Malow has a complete bid and labor availability to perform the work 
during the month of July/early August.   In order for material to arrive by 
July 1st, a purchase order for the material will need to be released right 
away.  Shop drawings can be available by May 30th and the lead time for 
material is 4-6 weeks. The trim and door frames can be installed in 4-6 
weeks.

10. BUSINESS 
A.  CONSIDERATION:  Resolution to Commemorate the Ratification of 
the 19th Amendment of the U.S. Constitution, 
Giving Women the Right to Vote  
Oakland County Board of Commissioners has asked school boards to assist 
them in commemorating an important milestone in our nation’s history – the 
passage of the 19 th Amendment guaranteeing and protecting women’s 
constitutional right to vote.  Districts that submit resolutions will receive a 19th 
Amendment Suffragette Victory Flag to be flown June 10-14, 2019 in honor of 
the 100th anniversary of Michigan’s ratification of the 19 th Amendment.  The 
flags will be flown again countywide from August 24 – 28, 2020 in honor of 
the 100th anniversary of the federal ratification.   
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
CELEBRATING THE 100TH ANNIVERSARY OF THE RATIFICATION OF 
THE 19TH AMENDMENT OF THE US CONSTITUTION,  
GIVING WOMEN THE RIGHT TO VOTE 
WHEREAS women of every race, class, and ethnic background across America 
have made historic contributions to the growth and strength of the United States 
in countless recorded and unrecorded ways; and 
WHEREAS the women of this nation initiated the most significa nt women’s 
movement in history – the need for women’s suffrage; and 
WHEREAS despite the strong opposition, the inspiration and determination of 
women nationwide brought about the extraordinary accomplishment – the right 
for women to vote; and   
WHEREAS on June 10, 1919, Michigan women cast their vote when Michigan 
voters ratified the 19th Amendment to the U.S. Constitution, and   
219
WHEREAS the State of Michigan was the second state to ratify the 19 th 
Amendment, giving women the right to vote; and     
WHEREAS on August 26, 1920, voters across the nation secured the right for 
women to vote when the 19th Amendment to the Constitution of the United States 
was ratified, empowering American women and declaring for the first time that 
they, like men, deserve all the rights and responsibilities of citizenship stating, 
"…the right of citizens  
of the United States to vote shall not be denied or abridged by the United States 
or by any State on account of sex"; and 
WHEREAS August 26, 2020 will mark the 100 th anniversary of the passage of 
the National Suffrage Amendment, the 19th Amendment to the U.S. Constitution, 
guaranteeing women the right to vote; and 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that the [YOUR NAME HERE] remembers 
and celebrates the historic women who fought  for their right to vote and 
recognizes the courage and inspiration of these bold women to change the course 
of history. 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that [YOUR NAME HERE] will fly the 19 th 
Amendment Victory Flag June 10, 2019 through June 14, 2019 to commemorate 
the State of Michigan ratifying the 19th Amendment and August 24, 2020 through 
August 28, 2020 to commemorate the ratification of the 19 th Amendment to the 
Constitution of the United States. 
 Yes: 
 No: 
220
 10. B. 05.21.19 
10. BUSINESS 
B.  CONSIDERATION:  Resolution in Support of Proper Funding of  
                                       Education in Michigan  
The Tri-County Alliance and OCSBA are encouraging school boards to pass 
a resolution in support of the Governor’s budget to fund education in our 
state.  The Board is now being asked to pass the following resolution in 
support of proper funding of education in Michigan.   
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
Resolution in Support of Proper Funding of  
Education in Michigan 
WHEREAS, the Michigan Constitution requires the state to support a system 
of free public elementary and secondary schools as defined by law; and 
WHEREAS, the work of the non -partisan School Finance Research 
Collaborative (SFRC) has provided Michigan with comprehensive 
recommendations on how to change our approach to how we fund Michigan’s 
schools to fully prepare all students for careers and success; and 
WHEREAS, the SFRC and other school finance studies have pointed to the 
need for significant new funding for K-12 schools in addition to changing how 
those dollars are allocated to reflect the unique needs of each individual 
students;  
THEREFORE BE IT RESOL VED, the Troy School District believes that 
Governor Gretchen Whitmer’s budget recommendations put the needs of our 
students first and foremost and we encourage the Michigan Legislature to 
support them. 
THEREFORE ALSO BE IT RESOLVED, the Troy School District strongly urges 
the Michigan Legislature to pass, and the Governor to sign, the Fiscal Year 
2019-2020 state budget prior to July 1, 2019.    
 Yes: 
 No: 
221
